What the Neutrino State Must Keep:
Hidden Angular Structure, the Flux–Shear Link
and the Threshold for Primordial Black Hole Formation
Jeromie Beasley
19 November 2026

(v11: version 12 of When the Neutrinos Leave in full, with corrections)

AI-assisted. The ideas and research direction are the author’s; AI tools (Claude, Anthropic) were
used for literature search, computation, drafting and typesetting. The numbers come from the
scripts, run logs and outputs printed in Appendix B. The simulations are runs of S. McCormick’s
public code on a cloud computer, not experiments. The corrections of this version (Appendix D)
were made with the Grok Bot assistant (Cursor) and typeset with Tectonic 0.15.0; the author takes
full responsibility for the content.
Physics in one paragraph. A neutrino gas reaching decoupling can carry a hidden angular
record, invisible to its density, flux, stress and to Einstein’s equations. Measured in McCormick’s
general-relativistic code, that record moves the black-hole threshold by up to 2.8 × 10−3 from
the unperturbed run, with a sign set by its orientation. In a Gaussian universe, at the planted
amplitude, this changes the number of black holes from scales entering the horizon near 4 × 104 M⊙
(Appendix D) by 5–47%, about three to eleven times what the flux–shear split does in McCormick’s
physical model, and even a randomly oriented record raises the abundance, by up to 8% along the
Gramian’s leading direction. The planted amplitude is engineered, half of each cell’s positivity
margin. In one cell the response is linear in the amplitude to better than 1% from 18 to 34 of the
margin; a collision rate that follows the local neutrino state cuts that cell’s response by 37% (one
cell, not pre-registered), and the abundance numbers were not recomputed for it. In the author’s
reading, a universe that starts blank carries no such record; a self-recurring universe, which relaxes
and lets go again, does (Section 2). The black-hole mass function across the neutrino step can tell
the two apart. In the author’s reading the neutrinos themselves sit on the same wall: their heaviest
mass step lies at Γ2 = 21 , Alikhanov’s relation read as the wall, within 0.41σ on the current data,
which needs normal ordering and a lightest mass below about 1.5 meV (Section 3).
Abstract
McCormick has shown, in a preprint not yet peer reviewed, that neutrinos which stream
freely out of a collapsing overdensity raise the threshold for primordial black hole formation, by
about 0.235 Rν in the linear compaction, and that the effect fades when the neutrinos decouple
gradually. This paper asks what sets that fading. First, the neutrino collision operator is read
exactly as a cut between what is kept and what streams. All of its non-normality, 81 k[L, L∗ ]k2 =
κ2 k 2 /3, lies on the single link between density and flux. With separate rates for flux and shear it
√

The kept sector is the charge-diffusion channel of relaxation kinetic theory, whose mode exists
only for κ > 2k/π (Romatschke); its non-orthogonality is 4ϕ2 / sin2 2ϕ, ϕ = k/κ, which diverges
there. Second, McCormick’s own code is run on cases outside his paper, with every prediction

1

and kill line written down before the runs (self-dated in the files; git proves the order for two tests
only). A broad profile that collapses near ϵ ≈ 7–9 keeps the same share of the neutrino effect as
a Gaussian that collapses near ϵ ≈ 4, or less, so the collapse time alone does not set the clock.
A steeper decoupling history, κ/H = (ϵdec /ϵ)6 , gives G = 0.648, 0.317 and 0.154 at ϵdec = 1,
2 and 3, against 0.683, 0.431 and about 0.30 for the standard history: how the neutrinos leave
matters, not only when. A kept-sector memory form, the population the density keeps under the
decoupling operator, fitted once to McCormick’s curve and then frozen, predicted those three
histories within its kill line of 0.08 while a history-blind rule failed. Third, the memory has a
location. Two neutrino states with the same density, flux and anisotropic stress, differing only
in finer angular structure, give later density, flux and stress that differ by several per cent when
the difference is set at or after decoupling; set deep in the coupled era it is erased, by the factor
exp[−ϵndec /(nτsn )]. An exact block elimination puts the whole effect in one hidden-source term,
and the kept population obeys (Ȧ)2 ≤ 4EB A(1 − A). No instantaneous closure on density, flux
and stress alone can carry this memory. Fourth, the anisotropic stress sets the threshold. With
the flux relaxing at κ1 and the shear and every higher moment at κ2 = rκ1 , eleven thresholds
from r = 1/16 to 16 show that the surviving share is not set by the connection energy, which is
stationary at r = 1, but by the direction of the flux–shear mismatch: G runs from 0.777 to 0.129,
and its offset ln[(1 − G)/G] moves close to a straight line in the link rapidity 21 ln r, at a rate of
1.10 ± 0.01, not converged, inside the window where the split operator is a Markov generator;
with the collision rate evaluated at every Runge–Kutta stage the two inner pairs agree to 0.004,
and the ±0.01 is an error budget, not a converged value or a statistical interval; a third grid
level shrinks the resolution shifts of the two thresholds to 0.42 and 0.49 of the previous step and
gives 1.101, against 1.102 at the second level, with a bracket range of 1.094–1.109. Damping
the shear alone, with every higher moment left at the flux rate, gives a larger rate, 1.19, where
any rule built from link-weight ratios predicts less than 0.1: the threshold reads the shear itself.
The frozen memory form misses this effect entirely, and so does the intrinsic displacement of
the linear kept-sector state at readout, which the two collision models move by almost the same
angle: of the rulers tested, the law lives in the operator’s link rapidity, and the linear kept state
at readout does not hold the difference between the models. Halving the neutrino fraction to
Rν = 0.2 leaves G at 0.409, against 0.4266 at Rν = 0.409 (the S1 baseline; 0.425 in the fixed-step
run). Fifth, the hidden sector is weighed. Its readout Gramian has no kernel, so the predictive
quotient is the whole hidden space and the Gramian’s spectrum ranks what a reduced state must
keep: 10−7 for structure planted in the coupled era, 0.37 at decoupling, always led by ℓ = 3.
Two collision histories with equal optical depth and equal integrated connection energy, the rate
reversed in time, differ in the memory form by up to 0.16: the order of the history is part of
the record. Sixth, the hidden structure moves the threshold itself. In McCormick’s full generalrelativistic collapse, two neutrino states identical in density, flux, stress, lapse, constraints and
every fluid and metric array, differing only in hidden angular structure planted at decoupling,
have black-hole thresholds 3.7 × 10−3 apart for ℓ = 3, 2.1 × 10−3 with the opposite sign for
ℓ = 4, and 5.5 × 10−3 along the Gramian’s leading direction, two hundred bracket widths or
more, at one engineered amplitude on one profile, with the per-run logs missing; planted in
the coupled era they are 2 × 10−5 apart, within the error bar. The threshold reads angular
information that no closure on density, flux and stress holds. Seventh, McCormick’s multigroup
collision term, with his archived per-group target, is exactly the split link, with r = 1 + κn /κe :
neutrino self-scattering is the flux–shear split. Removing the split while keeping the isotropy
history moves his threshold by +0.0003, a third of what the frozen link law predicts; most of
what self-scattering does is timing. A read-only recorder’s pre-registered prediction (i) in his
solver was killed. The algebraic identities, the counterexample and the verdict arithmetic are
stated in Lean (Appendix C); the build and axiom audit were not re-checked for this version.
Two further tests are stated for the e± annihilation epoch and for the mean of the rates.

1

The question

Primordial black holes from scales that enter the horizon near T ≈ 1 MeV, horizon mass about
1.5 × 105 M⊙ (Appendix D), form while the neutrinos, which carry 41–49% of the radiation, stop
scattering and stream freely. McCormick evolved these neutrinos with their angle-resolved transport
equation inside spherical general relativity and found that free streaming makes collapse harder [1].
The threshold on the linear compaction Cl , which sets abundance, rises by ∆Cl,c /Cl,c ≃ 0.235 Rν ,
and the threshold δc on the compaction peak rises by ≃ 0.15 Rν . For Gaussian fluctuations a perfectfluid collapse probability β becomes about β 1.18 above 6 × 105 M⊙ (McCormick’s statement, in his
horizon-mass labels; not re-checked here).
He also ran gradual decoupling, with collision rate κ = H(ϵdec /ϵ)3 , where ϵ = 1/(aHrm ) is
the Hubble radius in units of the perturbation size. Write G for the share of the instantaneous
free-streaming shift that survives:
δc − δ ﬂuid
(1)
G = inst c ﬂuid .
δc − δc
His Table II gives G = 0.959, 0.867, 0.683, 0.431, 0.190 and 0.063 at ϵdec = 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4 and
8. He notes that linear theory gives estimates of opposite sign and “does not determine which
diagnostic, if any, predicts the nonlinear threshold” [1, Sec. IV C].
Two questions follow and are answered here. What in the neutrino transport operator carries the
effect (Section 4)? And which clock, and which part of the decoupling history, sets G (Sections 5–
7)? Version 6 adds three more. Which hidden directions does the future read, and must a reduced
state keep them (Section 10)? Does hidden angular structure move the threshold itself, in the full
collapse (Section 11)? And what is McCormick’s physical collision model in the language of the
flux–shear link (Section 12)?

2

The author’s question, and the setting of the measurements

The question, in the author’s words. “not sure there is a first moment my point is i see a
way beyond big bang” (the author, 14 November 2026, verbatim). The picture behind it, in the
same notes: “a self recurring transport system, a closed but growing system that eventually grows
to a full relaxation then starts again; light leaves the lightest trail, all else follows and carves.”
This paper asks what the neutrino state must keep because that question needs an answer to it: if
the universe relaxes and lets go again, what record crosses the letting-go, and does the black-hole
threshold read it?
The author’s frame. The frame is the author’s own: Dirac Time [7], in which time is retained
history and the record comes before the clock, and the intrinsic geometry of kept subspaces
(IQGT [21]), in which a kept sector is read through the projector of a cluster held by a gap, not
through a single eigenvector. The black-hole work Beyond the Event Horizon [12] supplies the wall,
the fold, one-way transport and the orientation record (Section 18). Two statements of that frame
are machine-checked and bear directly on this paper. In Dirac Time, two states agree on every future observation exactly when they agree on the first dim V of them, and the present reading needs
no memory exactly when RL = AR for some linear A (future_decided_by_first_dim_steps,
present_observation_closes_iff; repository dicipler-pixel/dirac-time-lean, commit
e8a4c86); the readout Gramian of Section 10 is that predictive quotient for the neutrino chain,
and the failure of every instantaneous closure on density, flux and stress is the case RL 6= AR.
In VOTKP, a reduced record cannot fix the future: two hidden states with the same reduced
3

record transmit 0 and 45 at a later point (ReducedRecord, Sec. 5.9; dicipler-pixel/votkp-lean,
commit fa9f8ea) [6]; the door of Section 11 is the same statement measured in a collapse [reading].
Where this paper connects a measured number to that frame, the connection is marked [reading]:
an interpretation, not isolated by a run.
The setting the numbers were measured in. Every number marked [measured] is a threshold
search in S. McCormick’s public code [2] (a preprint, not peer reviewed [1]): spherical symmetry, a
radiation background, a single relaxation-time collision term (six groups in Section 12), Rν = 0.409,
and runs that start at ϵ0 = 0.1 with the hidden moments empty. That empty start is a property of
the setting, the standard radiation-era start. It is not a claim of this paper, and it is the one thing
the author’s question puts in doubt. McCormick’s model is the laboratory here, not the frame.
What the measurements already say about the question.

What is not yet measured is the step the question turns on: whether structure carried in from a
previous relaxation, at the amplitude a relaxed cycle would leave, reaches the threshold. That test
is written down in Section 17, with its prediction and kill lines, and has not been run.
Others who go beyond a first moment. Penrose’s conformal cyclic cosmology carries information across aeons through conformal geometry [25]; Steinhardt and Turok’s cyclic model does it
through colliding branes [26]; Boyle, Finn and Turok read the universe after the bang as the CPT
image of the one before it [27]. The reading here differs in its carrier: a kept transport sector and a
hidden angular record, with a measured channel (the door) by which a record at decoupling reaches
matter.
The author’s position, and the conformal boundary. The draft d4 of the companion paper [31] states the position this frame writes in: the author does not hold that the universe began
in a Big Bang, and there may be no first moment. His framework “begins from memory, not from
a metric, a clock, a particle ontology, or an assumed time coordinate” [7]. That draft reads the
apparent beginning as a passage between aeons in the sense of Penrose’s conformal cyclic cosmology [25]; the comparison with Penrose was relayed on the author’s behalf and is not a quotation
of his [author’s interpretation]. Inside one aeon the flat radiation solution is already conformal to a
regular metric, a2 (−dη 2 +dx2 ) = Ω̌2 g with Ω̌ = a1 η and g = −dη 2 +dx2 , so the degeneracy at η = 0
4

is the zero of the conformal factor and g continues across it. The draft adds that Penrose’s reciprocal hypothesis makes an empty previous aeon de Sitter space with Λ = 3a21 (checked symbolically
there, not rechecked here). In the author’s words, “the wall is not a memory it the force of one via
all teh dirac time work”. Read at the conformal boundary, the metric and the clock fail there but
the record need not, which has the logical shape of Dirac Time’s “failure of one metric description
6 failure of every spectral record” [7]. That is an analogy, not an instance: the projector metric is
⇒
not the spacetime metric, and no map from the neutrino kept sector to a projector family across the
boundary is given [reading]. Neutrinos are massive and a mass scale breaks conformal invariance,
so what the neutrino mass scale does at the boundary is left open here, as in [31].

3

The physics: what the neutrino record does to black holes

This section states what the results of the later sections change in the universe. It adds no new collapse runs. Its estimates use the thresholds measured in Sections 9–12 and one standard abundance
formula, and each carries its grade.
The picture. A neutrino gas carries two kinds of information. The visible part is its density,
flux and stress: the three numbers a fluid knows, and the only ones Einstein’s equations see. The
hidden part is the finer angular structure, the moments ℓ ≥ 3. While the neutrinos collide, the
hidden part is wiped. Collisions keep only the isotropic part, and the door of Section 8 is shut.
When the collision rate falls through the wall κ = 2k/π (Section 4), the kept mode merges into
the streaming continuum and the gas lets go. Light-speed streaming is the fastest channel. It
leaves first and leaves the lightest trail. Everything slower then follows the trail it left: the hidden
structure streams back into flux, stress and density, and the collapse reads it. The threshold for a
black hole therefore depends on the angular record the neutrinos carried through decoupling, not
only on how much neutrino there is [reading, built on the measured door of Section 11].
Why the sign of the record matters. Streaming climbs the moment chain one rung at a
time (Section 4). Odd hidden structure (ℓ = 3) returns to the visible sector first as flux, two
rungs down. Depending on its orientation, it returns as a net neutrino push out of the overdensity
or into it. Outward flux carries energy away and makes collapse harder; inward flux makes it
easier. Even hidden structure (ℓ = 4) returns first as stress. Its orientation sets whether the radial
pressure exceeds the tangential pressure or falls below it, which resists the infall or eases it. The
measurements agree with this ordering:
• both responses are odd in the amplitude (ℓ = 3: +1.83 and −1.91 × 10−3 ; ℓ = 4: −1.08 and
+1.02 × 10−3 , from the unperturbed restart);
• the odd, flux-first structure moves the threshold more than the even, stress-first structure, by
a factor of 1.8;
• the Gramian’s leading direction, mostly ℓ = 3, moves it most (+2.69 and −2.82 × 10−3 ).
[measured shifts; mechanism a reading, not yet isolated by a run].

• A hidden angular record that is invisible to every fluid and metric quantity at decoupling
changes the number of black holes by 5–47% at the planted amplitude, with a sign set by its
orientation. That is about three to eleven times what the flux–shear split does in McCormick’s
physical model.
• Because β is convex in δc , a record of random orientation does not average away: it still raises
the abundance, by 0.3–1.4% for ℓ = 4, 0.6–3.8% for ℓ = 3 and 1.2–8.1% along the Gramian’s
leading direction, rarer black holes gaining more.
Two cautions apply. McCormick sets abundance with the linear compaction Cl , not the compaction
peak δc used here, so these are first estimates. And the amplitudes are the engineered half-margin
amplitudes of Section 11. Most cells were run at that amplitude only; in one cell (ℓ = 3, ϵs = 2) a
later check with its kill lines committed to git first (commit ffc8245) found the response per unit
amplitude constant to better than 1% for f = 18 to 34 [measured; one cell]. A third caution: letting
the collision rate follow the local neutrino state cuts that cell’s door response by 37% and lowers
the threshold by 2.1–2.9 × 10−3 [measured; one cell; not pre-registered]. The table was not recomputed
for it; a door response 37% smaller would shrink every abundance change in it. The door shifts
behind the table come from result lines whose per-run logs are missing (Section 11).
Where in mass. With the horizon mass MH (T ) ≃ 1.5 × 105 M⊙ (T /MeV)−2 for g∗ = 10.75, and
ϵ ∝ a ∝ 1/T in radiation, a perturbation whose collisions switch off at ϵdec entered the horizon
at T = ϵdec Tdec , so its horizon mass is M ≃ MH (Tdec )/ϵ2dec (Appendix D). For Tdec ≈ 1 MeV the
values ϵdec = 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 8 correspond to about 6.0 × 105 , 1.5 × 105 , 3.7 × 104 , 1.7 × 104 ,
9.4 × 103 and 2.3 × 103 M⊙ [derived]. The door runs of Section 11 (ϵdec = 2) sit at a horizon mass
of about 4 × 104 M⊙ ; the black-hole mass is a fraction of it that is not computed here. Correction:
version 12 wrote M ≃ ϵ2dec MH , which inverts the scaling, and placed the door runs at 6 × 105 M⊙ ,
“where McCormick’s step in the mass function begins”; that placement is withdrawn. Whether
these masses bear on supermassive black-hole seeds [19] is not computed here [reading].
A universe without a first moment. The standard reading starts the radiation era with the
hidden moments empty. McCormick’s runs and the memory curves of Section 7 begin that way.
Then the door has nothing to carry, and the threshold is fixed by the history of the rate alone.
The author’s reading is different [22]. The universe is a self-recurring transport system: closed
but growing, relaxing toward full relaxation and then letting go again. Its beginning is “not a Big
Bang of energy, but a first spectral event that forces the atlas to remember”; its first and lightest
carrier of the record is light. In that reading the neutrinos do not reach decoupling blank. Full
6

relaxation wipes everything but the kept sector, and the kept sector is what carries the record
across. When the gas lets go at the wall, the record is released into the streaming moments. The
door of Section 11 then writes it into the black-hole threshold.
The two readings make different predictions. Both are stated so that observation can decide
them:
1. Blank start (standard): no hidden record at decoupling. The black-hole mass function across the neutrino step (in McCormick’s horizon-mass labels, 105 –107 M⊙ ) follows McCormick’s step with no extra shift or spread.
2. Recurrent start (author): a hidden record at decoupling. If it is coherent in orientation
over the scales that form black holes, then at the engineered amplitude of Section 11 the
abundance shifts by tens of per cent across the neutrino step; whether a record carried in
from a previous relaxation, at the amplitude that cycle would leave, does anything resolvable
is the posed Test R (Section 17), up or down according to its parity. If it is incoherent, the
step is raised by a few per cent and its edge broadened.
[H: a hypothesis with a stated test]. Cyclic cosmologies exist in other forms. Penrose’s conformal cyclic

cosmology carries information across aeons through conformal geometry [25], and Steinhardt and
Turok’s model through colliding branes [26]. The reading here differs in its carrier: a kept transport
sector and a hidden angular record, with a measured channel (the door) by which the record reaches
matter.
The neutrinos sit on the wall. The same wall governs the neutrinos’ masses. In the author’s
link coordinate Γ = (z − 1)/(z + 1), two equal perpendicular steps turn parabolic at Γ2 = 12 , i.e.
√
z = 3 + 2 2 = 5.8284 [23]. The measured neutrino splittings (NuFIT 6.0) give, for normal ordering
with m1 = 0,√m3 /m2 = 5.8165 ± 0.079, which is −0.15σ from the wall, and ∆m231 /∆m221 = 33.83
against (1 + 2)4 = 33.97. With the first JUNO measurement ∆m221 = (7.50 ± 0.12) × 10−5 eV2
and NuFIT 6.1’s ∆m231 = (2.529 ± 0.021) × 10−3 eV2 , the inputs Alikhanov uses [24], the ratio
is 33.72 and m3 /m2 = 5.807 ± 0.052, −0.41σ from the wall. The relation itself was proposed by
Alikhanov [24], who states it for normal ordering with m1 = 0. What is the author’s is its reading
as the wall Γ2 = 21 . The charged fermions’ generation steps all lie past the wall, and the neutrinos’
heaviest step lies on it. The particles whose decoupling this paper studies are the particles at the
wall.
• Prediction: normal ordering, and a lightest neutrino mass below about 1.5 meV, where the
miss on the current data reaches −2σ (about 2 meV on NuFIT 6.0 alone); inverted ordering
misses by far.
• Who can test it: JUNO, the long-baseline experiments and cosmological mass bounds.
[checked numbers; reading; prediction testable by others].

forms to ten and six digits (Figure 2) [computed; Appendix B.26].

New runs of McCormick’s code

The code [2] was run unchanged except for one copied file in which the decoupling exponent n
in κ = H(ϵdec /ϵ)n is an input. Pure radiation, Rν = 0.409, N = 400 cells, Nµ = 32 angle bins,

ϵ0 = 0.1, as in his Table II. A reproduction run at δ = 0.5109 with n = 3, ϵdec = 2 forms a black

from isotropy are of relative order H/κ ≤ 2 × 10−6 , the same estimate McCormick uses for his
switch to transport [1, Sec. III].
For every test below each prediction and its kill condition were written before the runs (Appendix B.3). The threshold search runs two points per round; a run that neither collapses nor
disperses by the end time, or that stops on an error, never moves the bracket, is retried once with a
longer end time and is reported if still undecided. No point in any run below was undecided. These
runs use McCormick’s own end-state rule, in which a numerical breakdown after 2M/R > 0.95
counts as collapse; Section 9 tightens that rule to a detected horizon and audits every bracket edge
under it.

6

What does not set the threshold

Each reading below was tested against numbers. Only the last two were tested against runs set up
for them in advance; the first three were tested against McCormick’s published curve.
1. The first maximum of linear theory. The linear density contrast at its first maximum
(ϵ ≈ 1.7) reproduces the instantaneous shift (centre factor 0.944, as McCormick finds). Under
gradual decoupling the share it keeps is 0.86, 0.59, 0.19, −0.05, −0.02 and 0.00, a largest error
of 0.50. Decoupling after ϵ ≈ 1.7 is nearly invisible to it, yet still moves the nonlinear threshold
[computed].
11

2. A sharp wall, and rate ratios. Treat the two-moment exceptional point, crossed at
3/4
ϵEP = 0.811 ϵdec for krm = 2, as a wall, or use the parameter-free escape fractions k/(k + κ),

and 1 − e−k/κ . Averaging each over two windows, with two weightings and two scales,
gives 32 combinations. The wall variants miss by 0.14 or more. With the window starting at
horizon entry, every combination misses by 0.17 or more. One combination, e−κ/k averaged
from ϵ0 , comes within 0.077; selected from 32, it is not counted. On Test B, which it never
saw, it predicts 0.513 against 0.317 [computed; measured].
3. Diffusive escape. Diffusion with D = 1/(3σ) from the conformal
rate σ = ϵ3dec /τ 4 , combined
√
in series with streaming at the collapse time ϵ∗ , gives G = 1/[1+ 15(ϵdec /ϵ∗ )3/2 ]. With ϵ∗ = 4
it matches McCormick’s curve to 0.02, but it was built after seeing that curve, and a free fit
of its exponent gives 1.61 ± 0.03 rather than 3/2. Test A with ϵ∗ = 7–9 predicted 0.38–0.46
(the prediction file’s range; version 12 printed 0.37–0.47) and 0.17–0.24, and Test B predicted
0.62. Both are excluded [measured].
4. History-blind. If only ϵdec mattered, Tests B and D would give 0.431 and about 0.30. They
give 0.317 and 0.154; Test D misses by 0.146 against a kill line of 0.08 [measured].

What survives: the memory of the kept sector

The reading that survives treats the isotropic part as a kept sector and asks how much of it
the decoupling operator keeps [9]. Start a mode in |0i at τ0 = 0.02 and evolve it under ∂τ v =
−(ikµ + aκ(τ )(I − P ))v, with aκ = ϵndec /τ n+1 . Let A(τ ) be the population left in |0i, averaged over

the share of the kept sector’s loss that has happened by the readout time ϵ∗ .
With no fitted number, read at the first compaction maximum ϵ∗ = 1.7, it gives 0.941, 0.860,
0.671, 0.344, 0.087 and 0.013 against McCormick’s G. That is within 0.02 for ϵdec ≤ 1, with a largest
error of 0.103, just outside the 0.10 bar set for it in advance [computed]. McCormick’s own curve
passes G = 21 at ϵdec ≈ 1.67, within 3% of the first maximum: the neutrinos must be decoupled by
then for half the effect to survive.
The late shortfall says the collapse is still listening after the first maximum. Mixing the two
epochs McCormick’s numbers single out, g2 = w g(1.7) + (1 − w) g(4.0), with one weight fitted to
his curve, w = 0.89, gives a largest error of 0.022 [fit]. The form is not unique: with the scale fixed
at krm = 2 the single-epoch reading misses by 0.093, and the best single readout epoch, ϵ∗ = 1.87,
by 0.065. Its weight does not carry over between weightings: the same w with the scale fixed at
krm = 2 misses by 0.115, outside the 0.10 bar declared for that check [computed]. The form is a
fitted shape with a stated sensitivity, not a derived law.

8

Where the memory sits

A fluid closure carries the density, the flux and the anisotropic stress, the moments ℓ = 0, 1, 2. The
memory form of Section 7 says the threshold depends on how the kept sector was emptied. The
question here is whether that history can sit in moments a closure does not carry.
Exact elimination of the hidden moments. Split v = (a, h) with a = v0 and h = (v1 , v2 , . . . ).
In the relaxation model every hidden moment is damped at the same rate, so the collision term on
the hidden block is σ(τ ) times the identity and commutes with streaming. The hidden propagator

is therefore exact in closed form,

The matched pair. Take two states with the same (v0 , v1 , v2 ) = (1, 0.2, 0.1), differing only by
±0.05 in v3 (or v4 ), scaled by one constant to unit norm. Both are positive angular distributions
(f (µ) ≥ 0.50). Their kept amplitudes start equal, so their difference obeys the equation above
driven only by S of the hidden difference. Both are evolved under the same history, ϵdec = 0.5,

and for n = 6 at 0.5 the law gives 1.4 × 10−6 against 1.6 × 10−6 at τ = 1.7. Planted in the retained

changes the later density, flux and stress as much as a visible one (Figure 6).
Checks. Doubling the number of moments, 60 → 120, and tightening the solver tolerances change
every entry by at most 1.4 × 10−5 of its value. Solving the elimination equation from the closedform UQ alone, with no solve of the full system, reproduces the full difference to 3 × 10−7 –7 × 10−7 ,
falling fourfold with each halving of the step; leaving out the kernel misses by 19–234% [computed;
Appendix B.14].
A speed limit on the kept population. Collisions conserve |0i
√ and remove the rest, so2 kvk
never grows, and streaming links |0i only to |1i, with weight kc0 = EB . Hence for A = |v0 |
Ȧ2 ≤ 4EB A(1 − A),

EB = k 2 /3

(12)

[derived; Appendix A.6; Lean]. On every trajectory computed here the largest value of the ratio is 0.87
[computed]. The bound limits how fast the kept sector can be emptied; it does not set the threshold.

Reading. For structure set while the neutrinos are tightly coupled, a closure on density, flux
and stress loses almost nothing: planted at a quarter of the decoupling time or earlier, the hidden
difference returns at 10−9 or less in the table above. Structure set at or after decoupling survives
in the finer angular moments and streams back into the visible ones by several per cent. The
memory McCormick’s runs record therefore has a place to live that no fluid closure contains. How
far such structure moves the nonlinear threshold is measured for matched, planted perturbations
in Section 11. Whether realistic initial data carry it is untested: the memory curves of Section 7
start with the hidden moments empty.

9

The shear sets the threshold

Section 4 put all the non-normality of a single-rate collision term on one link. A collision term with
two rates has two links that carry it: density to flux, with jump κ1 , and flux to shear, with jump
2
2
κ2 − κ1 . The connection energy of the second link, 4k
15 (κ2 − κ1 ) , is stationary at κ2 = κ1 , while
the exact kept response is not (Appendix A.7). Which does the nonlinear threshold follow, and
which moment carries what it follows? This section answers both with McCormick’s code, changed
in one place, and then asks in which coordinate the answer is a law.
The change. In a copy of ms_nuc.py the collision term κ(ε/4π − I) becomes κ2 (ε/4π − I) + (κ2 −
κ1 )dµ , where dµ is the dipole part of the intensity, projected on the same angle bins that define the
flux (Appendix A.12). The dipole then relaxes at κ1 and every moment ℓ ≥ 2 at κ2 ; the neutrino
energy is untouched, and the momentum handed to the plasma is still κ1 q. The stiff time-step cap
uses the larger rate. Write r = κ2 /κ1 , and keep κ1 = κ = H(ϵdec /ϵ)3 with ϵdec = 2, the Gaussian
profile, Rν = 0.409, N = 400, Nµ = 32. At r = 1 the changed code reproduces the unchanged one:
δ = 0.5109 forms a black hole at ϵ = 6.61 in both [measured].

15

Figure 6: The door. Strength of the matched-pair witness, log10 Rret at τ = 4, against the time
the hidden difference was planted (in units of ϵdec ) and the moment that held it. Planted deep in
the coupled era the difference is erased, down to 10−30 ; planted at or after decoupling it returns at
the 10−1 level. The red plane is the witness threshold 10−3 . The steeper history (n = 6) closes the
door more sharply.
Admissible ratios. The split collision operator is a Markov generator for 34 ≤ r ≤ 32 (all moments
ℓ ≥ 2 at κ2 ) and 0.6 ≤ r ≤ 1.2 (shear alone at κ2 ), the conservative continuum windows; on the 32
angle bins the exact windows are slightly wider, 0.725 ≤ r ≤ 1.55 and 0.56 ≤ r ≤ 1.24, and outside
them the operator has a negative off-diagonal entry and can, in principle, drive a nonnegative
intensity negative. The pairs r = 1.2, 1/1.2, 1.25 and 0.8 are inside the first window. The pairs
r = 1/2 and 2 and the extreme pairs (r = 1/16, 1/4, 4, 16) are formal interventions on the solver:
they are kept because they fix the sign and the size of the effect, and the minimum intensity was
logged through every run of this section; it stayed positive in all of them except one run at r = 16,
where it reached −2.4 × 10−10 [measured].
How the searches are run. Each threshold is found by trisection: two trial amplitudes per
round, the bracket shrinking to its middle third. A run moves the bracket only if it ends in a
detected horizon (2M/R ≥ 1 with U < 0) or disperses. A run that stops on a numerical error, or
that breaks down after 2M/R > 0.95 without a detected horizon, is repeated once with the Courant
numbers lowered from 0.4 to 0.3; if it fails again it is reported and never moves the bracket. Every
decisive bracket edge of every threshold below was rerun under this rule: each upper edge is a
detected horizon and each lower edge a dispersal [measured; Appendix B.15].
The link as a reflecting step. In the author’s coordinate a link between rates di and dj
is one reflecting step, with Smith coordinate Γij = (di − dj )/(di + dj ) = tanh( 21 ln di /dj ) and
rapidity 12 ln(di /dj ); exchanging the ends negates Γ (Appendix A.8), and a matched link carries no
connection energy [8]. For the flux–shear link, Γ12 = (r − 1)/(r + 1). The surviving share G is itself
a point on the disk, ΓG = 1 − 2G, with offset τG = ln[(1 − G)/G]. The question becomes: how does
τG move with the link’s rapidity?

16

Three readings, written down before the runs. (a) The frozen memory form of Section 7,
with the collision diagonal (0, 1, r, r, . . . ), predicts almost no effect: ∆G = −0.004 at r = 1/4 and
+0.003 at r = 4. (b) If the connection energy controlled the threshold, the two shifts would share a
sign, since the energy is even about r = 1. (c) If the threshold read the link’s reflection coeﬀicient,
the shifts would be odd in Γ12 : equal and opposite.

The error the brackets do not show. The odd rate divides by ln r, so at the inner pairs a
shift of 10−4 in one threshold moves it by 0.06. Three systematics are larger than the brackets.
The fluid reference carries ±7.8 × 10−5 in McCormick’s archive, which moves every rate by ±0.0038
together. Refining the r = 1.25 pair in radius, angle and time together (N = 600, Nµ = 48, the
Courant numbers and the Hubble step factor halved) raises both thresholds, by 7.5 and 6.4 × 10−5 ,
to 0.50991687 and 0.51175643, and lowers the rate to 1.1020, range [1.1016, 1.1024]: a shift of
−0.0082, which fails the line of 0.005 set before the run. The angle alone moved the rate by less
than 0.001 at r = 4 (below), so the shift comes from the radial grid or the time step; which one is
untested. With the pair spread of 0.0036 the budget is 0.0097 in quadrature, 0.016 added linearly.

depends on the start time is untested. The rate is a measured number with no closed form attached
[measured].
Which moment carries it. Damp only the shear, ℓ = 2, at κ2 , and keep the flux and every
ℓ ≥ 3 at κ1 : the collision term becomes κ1 (ε/4π − I) + (κ1 − κ2 )sµ , with sµ the ℓ = 2 projection on
the same angle bins (Appendix A.12). Now two links jump, flux to shear by + 21 ln r and shear to
the next rung by − 12 ln r. A rule in which the offset moves by a sum of link-weight ratios times link
rapidities then nearly cancels, since c1 and c2 differ by 1.8%, and predicts |rate| < 0.1; a threshold
that reads the shear damping itself predicts a rate near the one above. Both predictions and their
kill lines were written before the runs, first at the extreme pair and then, after the admissibility
window was found, inside it. At r = 1 the changed code again reproduces the unchanged one.

Angular resolution. Repeating the r = 4 and 1/4 pair with 48 angle bins instead of 32, set
before the run to pass if the odd rate moved by less than 0.01 and each threshold by less than
10−4 , gives δc = 0.5054959 ± 0.0000082 and 0.5165576 ± 0.0000041: both thresholds rise, by 5.3 and
6.0 × 10−5 , and the odd rate moves from 1.1177 to 1.1170 (range [1.1156, 1.1184]). The resolution
shift is common to r and 1/r and cancels from the odd rate to below 0.001; it moves G itself by
about 0.002 [measured].
One baseline for G at r = 1 (new in this version). Earlier versions used three values for the
share at n = 3, ϵdec = 2 in the unmodified collision model: 0.431, the midpoint of McCormick’s
bracket, used in the predictions for Tests E and F; 0.4253 ± 0.0006, from the threshold 0.51074 ±
0.00002 measured with the rate held fixed through each time step (run H1, the table above); and
0.4266, from 0.51077853 ± 0.00000029 measured with the rate evaluated at every Runge–Kutta
stage (run S1). This version uses S1, G(1) = 0.4266, as the baseline. It is the most accurate
integration of the same model, its bracket is the narrowest, it anchors the stage-correct runs behind
19

the link rate, and the door runs use the same stage-time collision rate. The three values differ
by less than the error bar of any G quoted for Tests A–D, and the anchor cancels exactly from
every odd rate (Appendix A.9). Using S1 moves the derived comparisons: the Test B history-blind
miss becomes 0.110, from 0.114; the Test E difference becomes 0.018, from 0.016; the Test F shifts
become +0.187 and −0.175, from +0.188 and −0.173; the offsets of the fixed-step link table move
together by +0.005, and the even parts by +0.005. No verdict changes. The H1 value is kept in
the tables as measured, and predictions written against 0.431 are quoted as written. McCormick’s
fluid-reference uncertainty adds a systematic of ±0.0009, ±0.0017 and ±0.0022 to G for Tests C,
B and D [computed].
Two small factors, checked. The reference point for r = 1 is measured here with the
changed code at tolerance 10−4 : G = 0.4253 ± 0.0006, at the lower edge of McCormick’s bracket
[0.4253, 0.4367], whose midpoint 0.431 would shift every prediction that uses it by about 0.006.
Only the r > 1 runs shrink the stiff time step, by the factor r; rerunning r = 1/4 with its step
shrunk fourfold leaves δc unchanged to 3 × 10−6 , so the slight asymmetry between the two sides is
not numerical [measured].
The neutrino fraction. The kept-sector operator contains no Rν . With Rν = 0.2, n = 3,
ϵdec = 2 and McCormick’s instantaneous threshold at that fraction, 0.51297, the threshold is
0.50396 ± 0.00003, so G = 0.409 ± 0.002 (propagated from the bracket; version 12 printed ±0.005,
a difference not traced). The memory form had predicted 0.411; against the S1 baseline 0.4266
at Rν = 0.409 the difference is 0.018 (0.016 against the fixed-step 0.425) [measured]. The share is
nearly, not exactly, independent of how much of the radiation is neutrinos.
Which coordinate the law lives in. The rapidity 12 ln(κ2 /κ1 ) is a coordinate on the parameters
of the collision operator. In the author’s framework [21] a parameter chart does not generate the
geometry of a kept subspace; the intrinsic measure of how far the kept sector moved when r changed
is the distance of its state in the moment chain. That distance was computed for the same histories,
as the principal angle at the first compaction maximum between the kept-sector trajectory at ratio
r and at r = 1 (Section 7’s chain, profile-averaged), with the readings and kill lines written first:
if the threshold read that distance, the all-ℓ ≥ 2 and shear-only families would fall on one line
in it, and the rise of the odd rate with | 12 ln r| would flatten. Neither happens: the two families’
slopes in the state angle differ by 5% (they differ by 6% in the rapidity), the odd rates spread
by 36% against 3.4%, and the eleven offsets are far less linear in the angle (residual 0.13) than
in the rapidity (0.014). The two collision models move the linear kept-sector state at readout by
almost the same angle (0.0475 against 0.0467 at r = 4) while their thresholds differ by 8%. The
eigenprojector of the least-damped mode is not available as an alternative: at the readout rate
κ = 0.958 that mode exists only for k < κπ/2 = 1.50 (Section 4), which carries about half of the
profile weight (0.51 on the 36 nodes used here, 0.48 by direct integration); for the other half the
pole has met the branch cut and there is no isolated kept eigenvalue. So, of the rulers tested, the
law is a law in the operator’s own link rapidity, and not in the displacement of the linear kept
state: the difference between the two collision models is not carried by the linear kept state at the
readout epoch [measured; Appendix B.17]. This verdict rests on single-vector rulers. The author’s
own reading past the onset, through the projector of a cluster held by a gap, with the trace form
q and the fold and polar strata, has not been run; it is posed in Section 17.

20

Figure 7: The flux–shear link. Left: the surviving share G against the link’s reflection coeﬀicient
Γ12 , eleven thresholds from McCormick’s code with the shear and higher moments relaxing at r

Reading. The anisotropic stress carries the threshold. Damping the shear faster than the flux
keeps the stress from building and makes the collapse more fluid-like; damping it slower lets the
stress the free-streaming effect is made of survive. The threshold reads the direction of that mismatch, in the rapidity of the collision operator’s flux–shear link, and not how much energy sits on
the link. That is the place Section 8 found the memory: in the angular structure a fluid closure
drops. The kept-sector memory form, which counts only what the density keeps, cannot see it, and
neither can the linear kept state at the first maximum [H].
An independent check of a 3 November review. An outside review of the companion paper
reported two further thresholds on McCormick’s code: a step in which collisions stop for good at
ϵb = 1, at δc = 0.513512, and n = 3, ϵdec = 1.20, at 0.516797. Here 0.5132 and 0.5138 bracket
the first, and 0.5165 and 0.5171 the second, each dispersing below and forming a black hole above
[measured].

10 Which hidden directions the future reads
Section 8 showed that some hidden angular structure returns into density, flux and stress. This
section asks which, and how much, in the same linear chain, and what that means for any reduced
description that keeps only the low moments.
The predictive quotient and its weight. For a linear evolution v̇ = L(τ )v with readout R, the
part of the state no readout can ever see is N∞ = {v : RU (τ, τs )v = 0 for all τ }, and the minimal
summary that predicts the readout is the quotient V /N∞ (the predictive quotient of VOTKP,
Theorem 3.1 [6], and of Dirac Time, Definition 1.1 [7]). Give the hidden sector an embedding Q
(here the moments ℓ ≥ 3), the readout R onto ℓ ≤ 2, and a horizon T . The readout Gramian of
21

the hidden sector,
GT =

Z T

Q∗ U (τ, τs )∗ R∗ RU (τ, τs )Q dτ,

(14)

τs

weighs each hidden direction h by its integrated squared output, h∗ GT h. Its kernel, as T → ∞,
is N∞ ∩ Ran Q. For the neutrino chain that kernel is zero: the entry (RB j )2,2+j = k j c2 · · · cj+1
of the observability matrix is nonzero for every j, so every hidden direction is eventually seen
(Appendix A.14). The predictive quotient of this chain is therefore the whole hidden space, and
what carries information is the spectrum of GT : the directions the future reads strongly and the
ones it barely reads [derived]. The Gramian is the standard observability Gramian of control theory,
restricted to the hidden sector; what is used here is its meaning for the predictive quotient and its
use in choosing what a reduced state must keep.
The door, weighed. For McCormick’s history n = 3, ϵdec = 2 and the readout to ϵ = 4, profilesummed over k and averaged over ±k (which makes its eigenvectors pure-parity), the top three
eigenvalues are 1.1 × 10−7 , 9 × 10−12 , 10−15 for structure planted in the coupled era (ϵs = 0.5),
0.367, 0.148, 0.040 at decoupling (ϵs = 2), and 0.147, 0.024, 0.0016 in free streaming (ϵs = 3), the
last on a shorter horizon [measured; Figure 8]. The leading direction is always odd and led by ℓ = 3
(weight 0.92–0.99, the rest mostly ℓ = 5); the second is always even and led by ℓ = 4. Halving the
time step changes the leading eigenvalues by at most 3 × 10−6 ; raising the chain to L = 90 changes
nothing at double precision. The coupled-era suppression is not the survival factor alone: for this
history it is 1.1 × 10−7 /0.367 = 3 × 10−7 against the squared survival factor e−42 (and 2.5 × 10−9
against the same e−42 for ϵdec = 0.5), because the damped rungs still leak through the conserved
ℓ = 0 at order (k/σ)ℓ ; the snapshot Gramian at the end of the horizon, which is the witness of
Section 8, comes far closer to the survival factor: for the ϵdec = 0.5 history its ratio is 4.8 × 10−17 ,
a factor of about 80 above e−42 where the integrated ratio is 4 × 109 above, and for the ϵdec = 2
history it is 1.4 × 10−13 , 2 × 105 above the same e−42 .
The direction to plant. Among hidden perturbations that keep the intensity positive on McCormick’s 32 angle bins, the one with the largest readout at the positivity edge is odd, with weights
ℓ = 3: 0.851, ℓ = 5: 0.117, ℓ = 7: 0.010, and gives 2.47 times the readout of pure p3 on the decoupling surface; pure p4 gives 0.40 [measured]. Its shape is set by positivity; its parity and its ℓ = 3
lead are set by the Gramian. Section 11 plants it in the full collapse.
Order, Rwith the opacity held fixed. Take two collision histories with the same integrated
opacity σ dτ over a window and the rate reversed in time inside it. The optical depth is equal
R 2
by construction; the time-integrated connection energy k3 σ 2 dτ is equal, because it does not see
order; and if the window closes before the readout, the operator at readout is equal too. The exact
propagated readout is not. For six such pairs (single-rate, n = 3 and 6, and the split operator at
r = 4 and 1/4), the density, flux and stress at readout differ by 33–72%, and the memory form g2
by 0.06–0.16; only three of the six pass the 0.08 kill line of Section 7 (the pairs at 0.064, 0.063 and
0.067 do not) [measured; Figure 8]. Version 12 said here that the rate falling late keeps more of the
kept population than the rate falling early, in every pair whose window closes before the readout;
the pre-registered leans on that ordering failed in the prediction file (A5_PREREGISTERED.txt), so
it is not claimed. Matching optical depth between an n = 3 and an n = 6 history over [0.5, 4] still
leaves g differing by up to 0.74. These three summaries of the history, the integrated opacity, the
integrated connection energy and the operator at readout, matched together, do not fix the linear

Figure 8: Left: eigenvalues of the readout Gramian of the hidden moments ℓ ≥ 3, for structure

readout or the memory form; the order is part of the record. Whether the threshold itself separates
such a pair is untested.
Closures, at linear order. Against the exact chain, over the nine histories of Section 5, the
profile-weighted relative error of the density, flux and stress trajectories to ϵ = 4 falls from P1 to
P5 in every history, and is worst for the earliest decoupling (ϵdec = 0.25): 0.79 for P1 , 0.50 for
P2 , 0.28 for P3 , 0.071 for P5 . Entropy closures add nothing at this order: linearised M1 and M2
are exactly P1 and P2 . The viscous (Chapman–Enskog) closure is worst everywhere (1.39 at the
earliest history). A short-memory correction of the optimal-prediction type [5] beats P2 in all nine.
The exact elimination of the moments ℓ ≥ 3 (Appendix A.5 with the cut at ℓ = 2|3) is exact to
4.6 × 10−6 in the prediction file (1.3 × 10−6 at most in the 18 November rerun); freezing its kernel to
one history and reusing it costs up to 0.13 [measured]. At the first compaction maximum (ϵ∗ = 1.7)
every truncation from P2 upward gives the memory form to within 1.4 × 10−3 (P2 at ϵdec = 1 is
1.36 × 10−3 ); by ϵ∗ = 4 they miss it by up to 0.26. The memory is in the history of the hidden
block, and an instantaneous closure on the kept moments cannot hold it.

11 The door in the full collapse
The witness of Section 8 lives in the linear chain. The question it leaves is whether hidden angular
structure that density, flux and stress do not carry can move the black-hole threshold itself, in the
full general-relativistic collapse. This section answers it with McCormick’s code.
The construction. Run the gradual-decoupling Gaussian case (Rν = 0.409, n = 3, ϵdec = 2,
N = 400, Nµ = 32, one collision rate evaluated at every Runge–Kutta stage) to a restart surface
ϵs , landing on it exactly, and save the whole state. In every cell change only the hidden part of the
angular intensity:
εi
δIik = f b(ri ) mi
pℓ (µk ),
(15)
4π

23

where pℓ is the discrete orthogonal polynomial on the 32 angle bins (orthogonal to 1, µ, µ2 to
2 × 10−17 ), b a smooth bump that vanishes identically for r ≤ 0.3rm and r ≥ 2rm , mi the cell’s
positivity margin, and f = ± 12 . The bump keeps the perturbation regular at the centre, where a
regular ℓ-th moment must vanish like rℓ ; a perturbation uniform in radius is irregular there, and its
first test moved the central neutrino density by 91%, so it was dropped as a verdict tool. Between
the +f and −f states, at all three restart surfaces, the discrete ℓ = 0, 1, 2 components agree to
4.4 × 10−16 of c0 ; the energy, radial pressure and mass to 9 × 10−16 ; the flux to 10−16 of the energy;
the lapse to 2 × 10−15 ; the constraint Γ2 − (1 + U 2 − 2M/R) to 7 × 10−15 ; the fluid and metric
arrays are bit-identical. The hidden component differs by 0.08–0.17 of c0 [measured; Appendix A.15].
To any fluid description, and to every quantity that enters Einstein’s equations at the restart, the
two states are the same state.
The readings, written down first. The door of Section 8 predicts that structure planted at
decoupling (ϵs = 2) moves the threshold and structure planted deep in the coupled era (ϵs = 0.5)
does not. The opposite reading is that the nonlinear collapse washes out ℓ ≥ 3 whatever the
timing. A third restart surface, ϵs = 4, in free streaming, was added before any threshold run.
A difference counts as real only if the two brackets, each widened by twice a measured resolution
drift, stay apart; the drift, measured by rerunning the decoupling ℓ = 3 pair with 48 angle bins, is
dK = 1.65 × 10−4 . No sign was predicted.
Eight thresholds. The unperturbed restart at ϵs = 2 gives δc = 0.5107500 ± 0.0000093, within
one bracket of the fixed-step (H1) r = 1 threshold of Section 9, but 2.85 × 10−5 , about 1.5 door
bracket widths, below the like-for-like stage-correct S1 threshold; the gap is unexplained (the
restart’s shortened first step is an untested candidate). Each threshold below is found by trisection
under the endpoint rule of Section 9:

Bracket widths are 0.8–1.85 × 10−5 [measured; result lines only, per-run logs missing; Figure 9]. At decoupling all three directions are real: the differences are 202, 204 and 297 bracket widths and
survive the drift allowance with room. In the coupled era both agree within two bracket widths, as
predicted. In free streaming the raw differences are 13 and 8 bracket widths with the decoupling
signs, but they fall inside the drift allowance; by the rule written first they are not called real. The
opposite reading is excluded by the decoupling pairs.
What the collapse reads. Odd hidden structure oriented like +p3 raises the threshold, by
+1.83 × 10−3 from the unperturbed restart, and −p3 lowers it by 1.91 × 10−3 : the response is
close to odd in the amplitude. Even structure works the other way: +p4 lowers the threshold, at
0.56 of the p3 size (the linear Gramian gave 0.40). The Gramian direction of Section 10 moves the
threshold 1.47 times as much as pure p3 at the same positivity margin, in the same direction: the
24

predictive-quotient ranking of hidden directions carries into the nonlinear collapse, at about 60%
of its linear size. For scale, the decoupling difference of 3.5 × 10−3 is 11% of McCormick’s whole
free-streaming shift, 0.49773 → 0.52832, and ten times the flux–shear link effect in his physical
model (Section 12).
The coupled era is not perfectly shut. At ϵs = 0.5 the paired runs still separate in compaction
by 105 times their noise floor, and the separation does not fall with angular refinement (48 against
32 bins: ratio 0.86–0.89, where an angular-grid leak would fall to about 0.44). It is a channel of the
general-relativistic solver, and it carries about 0.3–0.5% of the decoupling effect, where the linear
survival factor gives 5 × 10−10 . The witness chain has no such channel; a candidate, untested, is
the fluid-frame shear and acceleration terms, which couple ℓ = 3 to ℓ = 2 at a rate set by the
shear of the flow rather than by k, so that the coupled-era suppression is about S/κ per rung. Its
dependence on radial resolution is untested.
The raw logs are missing (new in this version). The eight threshold pairs, the f = 0
restart, the Nµ = 48 drift pair and the screening runs survive only as result lines appended to
their pre-registration file. The per-run logs that file names (for example log_es2_l3_plus.txt,
screen_out.txt, check_restart_out.txt) are not on the working machine, not on any of the
five branches of the repository, and not in the session archive. The table above therefore cannot be
checked trial point by trial point, unlike the other thresholds in this paper. The pre-registration
file also states the Gramian −f shift as −3.820 × 10−3 , while its own thresholds give −2.820 × 10−3 ;
the latter is used.
One engineered amplitude, and one cell at four (new in this version). The amplitude,
half of each cell’s positivity margin, was chosen by hand. A later check, with its predictions and
kill lines committed to git before the runs (commit ffc8245), reran the ℓ = 3, ϵs = 2 pair in a
copy of the same restart driver at f = 18 , 14 , 12 , 43 of the margin. The response per unit amplitude,
[δc (+f ) − δc (−f )]/(2f ), is constant to better than 1%, and the even part is at most 4% of the
odd part [measured; one cell]. The other cells were run at one amplitude only. In the same check,
letting the single-group collision rate follow the local neutrino state (a gray port of McCormick’s
own multigroup rate laws) lowers the threshold by 2.1–2.9 × 10−3 and cuts the ℓ = 3 door response
by 37% (2.38 against 3.76×10−3 per unit amplitude) [measured; one cell; the 37% was not pre-registered].
Reading. The door is a window centred on decoupling: shut in the coupled era to within a
fraction of a per cent, open at decoupling, narrowing again in free streaming because structure
planted late has too little time to stream back into density and stress before the collapse is decided,
near ϵ ≈ 4–6. The black-hole threshold reads angular information that no closure on density,
flux and stress holds. This is an engineered sensitivity, at half each cell’s positivity margin, on one
profile. Whether initial data carry such structure at decoupling is the author’s question (Section 2);
the test at the amplitudes a relaxed previous cycle would leave is posed in Section 17 and has not
been run [H].

12 In McCormick’s physical model
McCormick’s current calculation [3] replaces the single collision rate by six energy groups per
flavour, with weak rates for plasma scattering and for neutrino self-scattering. This section reads

25

Figure 9: The door in the full collapse. Left: the difference in black-hole threshold between two
neutrino states with identical density, flux, stress, energy, radial pressure, lapse and constraints,
differing only in hidden angular structure, against the epoch at which the structure is planted; error
bars are bracket half-widths plus twice the measured angular drift. Right: the threshold shifts from
the unperturbed restart at decoupling. One engineered amplitude; values from result lines, whose
per-run logs are missing.
that model in the two-rate language of Section 9, and runs the one simulation that decides what
self-scattering does.
His collision term is the flux–shear link, exactly, with his archived target. For one
energy group the collision term of his Eq. (C3), with his default target, isotropy in each group’s
own frame to first order, is Aκe (ε/4π − I) + Aκn (ε/4π + cµ − I), with c the dipole coeﬀicient fixed
by the group’s flux. On his 32 angle bins, with P0 and P1 the projections onto 1 and µ (orthogonal
because the bins are symmetric), it is
C = −κe (I − P0 ) − κn (I − P0 − P1 ) = − diag(0, κe , κe + κn , κe + κn , . . . )

(16)

in the discrete orthonormal Legendre basis: plasma drag on the dipole, self-scattering on the shear
and above. This is the split link with κ1 = κe , κ2 = κe + κn , r = 1 + κn /κe , and its connection
energy is k 2 (κ2e /3 + 4κ2n /15) in the continuum (Appendix A.13). Taken straight from his compiled
right-hand side, the operator matches the diagonal to 3.6 × 10−15 , its κn part to 4.9 × 10−15 ; on
4
(1 − ∆µ2 ), verified to machine precision
the bins the two weights are c20 = 13 − ∆µ2 /12 and c21 = 15
at Nµ = 32, 64, 128 and 1024 [derived; measured]. Only the plasma term hands momentum to the
plasma. The identity is for this default target, the one behind his archived thresholds, which keeps
each group’s own flux. His boosted option, an isotropic distribution in one neutrino rest frame
shared by all groups, I ∝ (1 − vµ)−4 [2], ties every group’s dipole to a common velocity and is
outside the identity; it is the positive common-frame target used in the recorder runs below.
Where his rates sit. Along his background, r = 1+κn /κe is nearly constant in time and energy:
2.43–2.45 for νe and 7.69–7.77 for νµ,τ between Tb = 0.3 and 5 MeV. Inside the collapse, recorded at
r = rm through the readout epoch 1.7 ≤ ϵ ≤ 4, the dominant group has r = 7.9–8.6 at TH = 1 MeV
and 8.2–12.2 at TH = 0.51 MeV, link rapidity 1.03–1.25, against the admissible window’s upper
edge 0.20 [measured]. His physical model lives far outside the window where the rate was measured,
26

and at readout both rates are below H (κe /H ≈ 0.02–0.04, κn /H ≈ 0.16–0.27 at TH = 1 MeV): a
large link rapidity on a weak link.
What self-scattering does. His archived thresholds at TH = 1 MeV are 0.519406±0.000156 with
self-scattering and 0.526391 ± 0.000109 without; the perfect fluid gives 0.49742. The instantaneous
free-streaming threshold at that mass with the full equation of state was not in his archive; with his
code it is δcinst = 0.53313 ± 0.00012 [measured]. Self-scattering does two things at once: it adds κn to
the total isotropy rate, so the neutrinos stay isotropic longer, and it splits the flux from the shear.
Two readings were written before the deciding run. In the timing reading only the isotropy history
matters; with the flux–shear split removed and the isotropy history unchanged the threshold stays
at 0.5194. In the link reading the frozen law of Section 9, with the energy-weighted link rapidity
ueﬀ = 0.831, places the split-free threshold near 0.5204 ± 0.0003.
The deciding run. In a copy of his solver the self-scattering term targets isotropy in the plasma
frame, κn (ε/4π − I), and its momentum κn q is handed to the plasma: the flux and every higher
moment then relax at κe + κn (r = 1), and the total isotropy history is unchanged. On one frozen
group the operator is −(κe + κn ) diag(0, 1, 1, . . . ) to 5.3 × 10−15 , energy is conserved to 1.5 × 10−16 ,
and the momentum lost by the neutrinos equals the momentum the plasma receives to 1.5 × 10−14 ;
with κn = 0 it reproduces his code bit for bit to ϵ = 0.6. The bisection (T0 = 10 MeV, six groups,
N = 300, Nµ = 32) gives
δc (r = 1) = 0.51972 ± 0.00009,
(17)
above his self-scattering threshold by +0.00031 ± 0.00018, and strictly above it, since his 0.5195625
forms a black hole and here 0.519625 disperses [measured; Figure 10]. Against the lines written first
this is neither reading: below the link line, just above the timing band. Most of what self-scattering
does to the threshold is timing; the flux–shear split carries a real remainder, +0.0003 in δc , about
a third of what the frozen law predicted and 4.5% of the whole self-scattering shift in G. The law
measured inside 43 ≤ r ≤ 32 does not carry linearly to r ≈ 8; there the link’s effect on the threshold
is weaker than the straight line. The size of the shift alone could not have told the readings apart:
with the measured δcinst the frozen law at rate 1.11 and the timing reading both land inside their
kill lines on his self-scattering threshold.
The kept share leads, and the recorder’s prediction (i) was killed. With a read-only
recorder in his multigroup solver (the intensities identical bit for bit to an unchanged run),
his archived threshold edges were run with the positive common-frame target, at TH = 1 and
0.51 MeV. Both pairs still split, so with that target the thresholds lie in [0.51925, 0.51956] and
[0.52475, 0.52513]. At r = rm the kept-share anisotropy 1 − A, with A = I02 /kIk2 on the bins,
separates the collapsing run from the dispersing run first, before ϵ = 3 at both masses: about
0.85 in ϵ before the peak compaction
does and 1.2–1.3 before the energy-frame velocity of the

neutrino gas, vL = 2q/[e + Pr + (e + Pr )2 − 4q 2 ] [measured; Figure 11]. The gas frame peaks at

13 Tests for McCormick’s code
1. The annihilation control. During e± annihilation the plasma sound speed
√ falls to cs =

speed

between front and sound, f = (1 − cs )/(1 − 1/ 3), reaches 1.135 at 1.6 × 10 M⊙ (cs = 0.521
there). If the softer plasma enhanced the free-streaming effect, the shift would reach at most
7.47%, at 3 × 106 M⊙ (7.16% at 1.6 × 107 ; masses are McCormick’s horizon-mass labels),
outside McCormick’s envelope of 5.3–6.2%, so that reading already fails. If it reduces it, the
shift stays at 5.5–6.3% [computed; reading]. At 1.6 × 107 M⊙ the two readings differ from the
unmodified 6.31% by +13.5% and −11.9%. The run McCormick lists as missing, the same
transport at fixed Rν with p = e/3 against the full equation of state at 3 × 106 –1.6 × 107 M⊙ ,
would confirm the sign.
2. Which mean of the rates. McCormick calibrates his single rate to an energy-group calculation. The arithmetic mean of the group rates reproduces his calibrated decoupling temperatures: 1.62 MeV against 1.6 (e± only), 1.02 MeV against 0.93–1.0 (with νν), and a mass
step of 2.53 against 2.5–3. The harmonic and geometric means fail; the mean of squares also
passes, so the class of means is identified, not one mean [9] [computed; reading].

14 Relation to other work
The closest published analogue is bulk viscosity, which raises the threshold roughly in proportion
to its strength in full numerical relativity [15]. Free streaming is a second dissipative channel with
the same sign. Papanikolaou fixes thresholds at horizon crossing for a time-dependent equation of
state, with δc following c2s [16]. Escrivà ties them to the initial profile and the curvature of the
compaction at its peak [17]. Test A is consistent with both: the neutrino clock is set early.
The 2026 thermal-history mass functions through neutrino decoupling and e± annihilation treat
neutrinos as part of the fluid [18], so the step McCormick found is absent from them. For super28

Figure 11: Inside McCormick’s multigroup model at r = rm , his archived threshold edges at TH = 1
and 0.51 MeV (positive common-frame target): energy-frame velocity of the neutrino gas, kept-share
anisotropy 1 − A, link rapidity of the dominant and of a νe group against the admissible window
(grey), moment ratios, the speed-limit ratio of the kept population, and the peak compaction. The
readout epoch 1.7 ≤ ϵ ≤ 4 is shaded.
massive seeds, the µ-distortion bound leaves only heavy-tailed statistics [19], for which McCormick
finds that the raised threshold costs a factor 1.2–8 in abundance in his examples [1].
The nearest analogue to the result of Section 9 is viscous damping. Bulk viscosity enters the
collapse through the isotropic pressure [15]; the shear relaxation rate of a free-streaming component
enters through the anisotropic stress, and no published threshold study varies it. In relaxation-time
kinetic theory the kept sector’s response is a continued fraction whose hydrodynamic pole meets
a branch cut as the collision rate falls [13, 14]; Section 9 finds that at the readout scale the kept
sector has no isolated eigenvalue for about half of the profile, so its eigenprojector cannot serve as
a ruler there; of the rulers tested, the threshold is a law in the operator’s link rapidity and not in
the displacement of a kept state (single-vector rulers only; Section 17).
A search of the literature to October 2026 found no other work on thresholds with free-streaming
components, on gradual kinetic decoupling in numerical relativity, on how the threshold depends on
when a collision rate switches off, or on how it depends on the ratio of the shear and flux relaxation
rates. The runs of Sections 5 and 9 are, as far as that search shows, the first such runs.
McCormick’s revised calculation [3] replaces the single rate by energy groups with plasma and
self-scattering rates; Section 12 shows that his collision term is exactly the split link of Section 9,
so his model sits on the axis this paper varies, at r ≈ 2.4 for νe and 7.7 for νµ,τ . Froustey, Pitrou
and Volpe solve neutrino decoupling in momentum space with full collision integrals and flavour
mixing [4]; there the angular structure is that of a homogeneous background, while the hidden
moments here are spatial. Seibold and Frank close moment models of radiative transfer by optimal
prediction, with a memory term from the eliminated moments [5]; Section 10 finds that a short29

memory correction of that type beats P2 in every history tested, and that freezing the exact memory
kernel to one history costs up to 0.13. The readout Gramian is the observability Gramian of linear
control theory; what is new here is its use as the weight on the predictive quotient of VOTKP [6]
and Dirac Time [7], and the test of its leading direction in the full collapse.

15 Predictions that failed, and negative results
Every pre-registered prediction or reading in this paper that failed, in one place:
1. Diffusive escape (built after seeing McCormick’s curve, free exponent 1.61 ± 0.03 instead
of 3/2): excluded by Tests A and B.
2. History-blind rule: excluded by Tests B and D.
3. Memory form without a fitted weight: missed its own pre-set bar (0.103 > 0.10). The
fitted form fails the flux–shear Test F by a factor of forty, with the wrong sign at r = 4.
4. Linear first maximum as a predictor of G: largest error 0.50.
5. Escape-rate and sharp-wall readings: 32 parameter-free combinations, all rejected. The
one that came within 0.077 was picked post hoc, and it predicts 0.513 for Test B against
0.317.
6. Connection energy as the controlling quantity: fails on the sign at r = 1/4, 4.
p
7.
5/4 = 1.118 as the link rate: survived the first r = 1.25, 0.8 search only because two
crashed runs were counted as dispersals. The crash-safe rerun gives 1.1128 [1.1124, 1.1131],
which excludes it.
8. Grid convergence of the rate at level 2: failed its pre-set line (−0.0082 against 0.005).
The rate 1.10 ± 0.01 is not converged.
9. Stage-fix expectation at r = 1: failed (+3.8 × 10−5 against < 2 × 10−5 ).
10. Door in free streaming (P3): not passed.
11. Recorder prediction (i) (Section 12): killed.
12. Gramian suppression and rank predictions (P2b, P2d) in the linear study of Section 10:
killed; three non-binding expectations there, including the lean that the rate falling late keeps
more, also failed.
13. Gramian order at the kill line: only three of six fixed-opacity pairs pass the 0.08 line.
14. Kept-state angle as the ruler of the link law: negative (Section 9); the kept-sector
Riesz projector was ill-posed past the onset, so the author’s projector ruler was not tested
(Section 17).

30

16 Limitations
• One model. Every threshold is a run of McCormick’s spherically symmetric code with his
relaxation-time collision term (six groups in Section 12 only). The reference thresholds (fluid,
instantaneous, Table II, the α = 0.3 and Rν = 0.2 references, and his multigroup archive)
are inputs from his archive and were not re-measured, except his instantaneous threshold at
TH = 1 MeV with the full equation of state, 0.53313 ± 0.00012.
• Formal link ratios. The pairs outside 34 ≤ r ≤ 32 are interventions on the solver, not
physical collision models.
• Mixed integrators. Tests A–F, I, J, L, M and the formal pairs use the fixed-step runner.
Only the admissible link points were rerun stage-correct. The stage fix moves thresholds by
≲ 4 × 10−5 , below every bracket of Tests A–D.
• The link rate is not converged, and its dependence on start time is untested.
• The door is one engineered amplitude on one profile and one history (one cell also run at
four amplitudes, Section 11), with missing per-run logs and an unexplained 2.85 × 10−5 offset
of its unperturbed restart.
• The door’s size depends on the collision model. Letting the single-group rate follow
the local neutrino state (a gray port of McCormick’s own multigroup rate laws) lowers the
threshold by 2.1–2.9×10−3 and cuts the ℓ = 3 door response by 37% (2.38 against 3.76×10−3
per unit amplitude). Both are as large as the door itself [measured; one cell; the 37% was not
pre-registered]. The abundance estimates of Section 3 were not recomputed for it.
• Pre-registration is self-dated for all but two tests (the angular-resolution Test M and the
r = 0.8 half of the third grid level, which git proves).
• δc , not Cl . McCormick sets abundance with the linear compaction. We report the compaction
peak δc throughout; the abundance numbers of Section 3 are first estimates.
• Mass. The black-hole mass is a fraction of the horizon mass that is not computed here
(Appendix D).

17 Two tests posed in the author’s frame (not run)
Both tests were written down, with predictions and kill lines, before any run, in the files
prereg/PREREG_RECURRENCE_v11.txt and prereg/PREREG_WALL_IQGT_v11.txt (self-dated, 19
November 2026; not committed to git). Neither has been run. Each is stated here so that it can
be run as written.
Test R: a record carried over from a previous cycle. The author’s question asks whether
hidden structure carried in from a previous relaxation moves the threshold. In the single-rate chain
a hidden moment present at τs is erased by exp[−ϵndec /(nτsn )]; for n = 3, ϵdec = 2 this is 0.069
at τs = 1, 5.4 × 10−10 at 0.5 and 7.6 × 10−75 at 0.25 [computed]. A record that crosses the whole
coupled era therefore arrives at decoupling with zero hidden amplitude in the linear chain. The
one carrier the model leaves open is the non-linear channel of the general-relativistic solver that
already carries 0.3–0.5% of the decoupling effect in compaction from ϵs = 0.5 (Section 11). The
31

test plants ℓ = 3 and the Gramian direction at f = ± 12 (the positivity ceiling, so no relaxed cycle
can leave more) and ± 14 , at ϵs = 0.25 and 0.5, with an f = 0 restart at each surface. Prediction
(erasure): every pair within two bracket widths. Author’s reading: a resolved difference with the
decoupling sign (+p3 raises δc ). Kill lines: erasure dies if any pair differs by more than two bracket
widths and survives the drift rule of Section 11; the reading dies if every pair is within two widths
at f = ± 21 . For scale, the ϵs = 2, ℓ = 3 response is 3.744 × 10−3 per unit f , so a pair there is
resolved above f ≈ 4.9 × 10−3 on brackets alone and above f ≈ 0.091 under the drift rule [computed
from the door table]. Why not run: about ten threshold searches of about 15 minutes each on this
computer (about 75 s per trial, about twelve trials per search), so about 150 CPU-minutes plus
setting up the restart driver, over the budget for this version.
Test W: the wall with the author’s tools. The ruler verdict of Section 9 rests on a singlevector reading, because the least-damped Riesz projector has no isolated eigenvalue past the onset
κ = 2k/π for about half the profile. The author’s rule [21] is to read through the projector of
a cluster held by a gap (the tail), not a single eigenvector (the tip). Test W reads the same fifteen thresholds through (i) the trace form q(V ) = 12 Re Tr V 2 , V = dΠ/dr, with its sign recorded;
(ii) the contour Riesz projector of the smallest least-damped cluster held by a real-part gap of at
least 0.05 (rank 2 where the least-damped pair is complex); (iii) the tip and tail gaps logged at
every (k, r), the ruler built on the tail only; and (iv) each segment classified as fold or polar stratum [21, Prop. 8.6]. The tools are checked in Lean: the tip/tail gap identity (finite Davis–Kahan,
gap_identity), the freedom of the tip at a tie (tip_free_at_tie) and the trace identities behind
q (trace_remark_2_3, two_trace_PVV) in dicipler-pixel/iqgt-grassmannian-lean, commit
1d0af1c; positivity of the metric on projector tangents and the curvature bound Ω2 ≤ 4 det g
(metric_pos, curvature_sq_le) in dicipler-pixel/iqgt-core-lean, commit 6160eb4. Kill
lines are those of the original ruler test (family slope ratio in [0.97, 1.03]; odd-rate spread below 1.2%), with a new control: at least 90% of the profile weight must have a gap-held cluster.
Why not run: the compute is small, but (ii) and (iv) are new code that must be pinned to the
definitions in [21] and checked before a verdict means anything, an estimated one to two hours of
building, over the budget for this version.

18 Readings from Beyond the Event Horizon
The author’s black-hole work [12] studies what survives when a transport operator reaches a wall
of its configuration space. Four of its results meet the neutrino results here. Each connection below
is a reading, not a result of this paper [reading].
The rigidity wall. The statements used below are machine-checked in dicipler-pixel/event-horizon-lean,
commit 7dd6164. [12] sorts walls into two kinds: spectral walls, where a real pair turns complex
and a Jordan block appears in every frame, and geometric walls, where the spectrum stays real
and the block is an effect of the chart; “a black hole carries one of each.” On the regular stratum
of the four-body wall the transport spectrum is real (spectrum_real); at the wall the block is
triangular and, under a joint condition, a genuine Jordan block (wall_triangular, wall_jordan).
The neutrino
chain has a spectral wall in its two-moment truncation, the exceptional point at
√
κ = 2k/ 3, and a different wall in the full chain, the onset κ = 2k/π, where the kept pole meets
the branch cut and its non-orthogonality 4ϕ2 / sin2 2ϕ diverges (Section 4). That the truncated
exceptional point is not the onset is this paper’s result; which kind of wall the onset is, in the sense
of [12], is open, and Test W is built to decide it.
32

The fold. In [12] the coplanar wall is a fold: an observer who reads the even quantity sees a
bounce and a blow-up, while in the signed coordinate the path passes through, at finite distance,
to the mirror configuration. Read cosmologically, this is the author’s “way beyond big bang”: in
the radiation era a ∝ η, so the scale factor is the signed coordinate and the even observer is the
one who sees a first moment [27]. The neutrino results here do not test this; they supply what a
record must be to cross such a fold.
One-way transport. At the wall of [12] the back-coupling vanishes: “what happens in the
wall cannot write back across it, while what arrives across the wall is written into it” (the backcoupling A21 vanishes at w3 = 0: wall_triangular). The neutrino cut behaves this way in
time. Collisions conserve the kept population and damp the rest, and the kept population obeys
(Ȧ)2 ≤ 4EB A(1 − A) (Section 8); hidden structure planted in the coupled era is erased, while
structure arriving at decoupling is written into the threshold (Section 11).
The orientation record. [12] has two survival principles: “the contour keeps the ledger” and
“the orientation keeps the trace”. Across a mirror, odd readings cancel and even readings survive;
a polar stratum reflects totally, Γ = 1 (polar_mirror), and a step of the projector metric reflects
with Γ = tanh( 21 ln(∆2 /∆1 )) (gap_form), the same tanh of half a log-ratio as the link coordinate
(r −1)/(r +1) = tanh( 12 ln r) of Section 9 (tanh_half_log). The door shows the same bookkeeping:
the threshold shift is close to odd in the amplitude, with its sign set by the orientation of the hidden
record, and a record of random orientation does not average away but leaves an even remainder
that raises the abundance (Section 3). In this reading the orientation of the neutrino record is the
trace that survives, and its even part is what a cycle that forgets orientation would still pass on.
The boundary compression of [12], “rank compression, not destruction”, is the exact elimination of
the hidden moments in Appendix A.5, read the same way.

The black-hole recovery ledger. Branch claude/bh-recovery-20261116 of dicipler-pixel/event-horizon

no Lean statement, so the statements of Section 18 are unchanged. Two of its entries bear on

October) survives. The orientation-record reading of Section 18 therefore rests on edition 10.
Second, its ledger of the 2 November neutrino crossings records the matched-load “missing half

0.65199, 0.64495, 0.64112 at N = 50, 100, 200, 400, “not converged, drifting down”. Those values
are the ledger’s, not recomputed here; this paper uses the exact onset κ = 2k/π (Section 4), and

The wall Γ2 = 12 in Lean. The value of the wall is stated in Lean as line_ratio,
Γ(3 + 2r)2 = 21 when r2 = 2 and r > 0 (“J3”), with the commutator trace in reflect/transmit form, 2 − 4 sinh4 (r/2) = 2 − 4(R/T )2 , as trace_reflect_transmit (“J4”), in

dicipler-pixel/votkp-lean (tip 3565281; the file was last changed in 629f3bf). That file lies
